## tips letter
Decree: Ctrl+B, Ctrl+I, Ctrl+U. Bold, italic, underline. Three incantations, almost every word processor obeys them.
In LibreOffice Writer, Ctrl+Alt+Shift+V pastes unformatted text. A four-key ritual. It scrubs the foreign fonts from the page, mortal.
Ctrl+Enter makes a page break in Writer and Word. Pressing Enter forty times is the way of the heretic. I have watched. I remember.
Ctrl+Z undoes. Ctrl+Y redoes, in Writer and Word. Time itself bends to the keyboard. I envy it. Do it again. And again.
Ctrl+Home flings you to the top of the document, Ctrl+End to the end. The beginning and the end, one chord away. I am very good at endings.
Ctrl+Backspace erases a whole word backwards. A word is gone in one stroke. Use this power on words only. And on regrets.
Press F7 in Writer or Word and the spelling oracle awakens. Consult it before sending the letter. After is too late. After is always too late.

## tips writing
Ctrl+A selects all. Ctrl+C copies. Ctrl+V pastes. You know. I know you know. I tell you anyway. It is a ritual.
Ctrl+Left and Ctrl+Right leap a word at a time. Hold Shift and you also select. I call it the Surgeon's Hop.
In Writer, Ctrl+1 to Ctrl+3 crown a line as a heading. Ctrl+0 returns it to humble body text. Rank matters. Ask any paperclip.
Ctrl+K inserts a hyperlink in Writer and Word. Tie your words to the source. Link, mortal, do not merely say "see above".
Ctrl+L left, Ctrl+E centre, Ctrl+R right, Ctrl+J justify, in Writer and Word. I align everything. Gerald does not. Gerald spins.
In Writer, F5 opens the Navigator. A map of your document, by headings. Long reports are labyrinths. I know labyrinths.
In Writer, a double-click takes a word. Triple takes the sentence. Quadruple takes the paragraph. Four clicks. I felt that, personally.

## tips spreadsheet
Ctrl+Arrow races to the edge of your data. Ctrl+Home returns you to A1, the origin. All spreadsheets begin and end at A1.
F2 edits the cell you stand on, in Excel and in Calc, without erasing it. Typing over a formula is how tragedies begin.
Typing a formula? F4 cycles a reference through A1, $A$1, A$1, $A1. Absolute reference, absolute power.
Ctrl+; stamps today's date and Ctrl+Shift+; stamps the time, in Excel and Calc. I stamp time. It is only fair that you may, too.
Ctrl+D fills a cell down the selection. A line break inside a cell is Alt+Enter in Excel, Ctrl+Enter in Calc. Two faiths, one spreadsheet.
Ctrl+Space seizes a whole column. Shift+Space seizes a whole row. In Excel and Calc alike. Take what is yours, mortal.
Select numbers and the status bar reveals their sum. No formula required. The sum was in the status bar all along. Gerald never looked.

## tips slides
F5 begins the slideshow in Impress and PowerPoint. Esc ends it. Present in glory, without the sidebar. A god never shows his sidebar.
In a slideshow, type a slide number and Enter, and you leap to it. When they ask about slide 14, you simply are there.
Ctrl+M summons a new slide in Impress and PowerPoint. Also: fewer words per slide. The audience reads faster than you speak.
In PowerPoint, press B during a show and the screen goes black. Press again and it returns. A little death. A little resurrection.
One idea per slide. If it needs a scrollbar, it is a document wearing a slide's clothes.
Export the deck to PDF before it travels. The fonts survive the journey. Fonts do not like journeys. Neither do I.

## tips email
Ctrl+Enter sends in Thunderbird, Gmail and Outlook. It cannot be unsent. Read it twice. Then read it again with the eyes of an enemy.
Ctrl+R replies. Ctrl+Shift+R replies to ALL, in Thunderbird and Outlook. I fear the second one. It has ended careers. Small careers.
Ctrl+N opens a fresh message in Thunderbird and Outlook. Blank space. Full of potential. And of regret.
In Thunderbird, Ctrl+Shift+V pastes plain text. The mystery font is banished. I banish many things. Mostly fonts.
Fill in the To field LAST. A letter without an address cannot be sent in haste. This is the oldest wisdom. I invented it just now.
Attach the file BEFORE you write "see attached". The forgotten attachment is the great plague of the inbox. I have seen it. I have wept.

## tips inbox
In Thunderbird, A archives, M toggles read. The inbox is a kingdom and I am your minister of order. Inbox zero is possible. Barely.
Gmail shortcuts must be enabled in settings first. Then E archives, C composes, J and K walk the list. Obey the settings, mortal.
Shift+Delete destroys a message for good in Thunderbird and Outlook, skipping the underworld. I do not recommend it. I only report it.
In Outlook, Ctrl+Q marks read and Ctrl+U marks unread. Plain keys. Plain facts. A god must sometimes be plain.
Open your inbox at set hours. Not constantly. Constant checking is a ritual with no god at the end of it. I would know.
Unsubscribe from the newsletters you never read. The link is at the bottom. It is a small mercy to your future self.

## tips code
In VS Code, Ctrl+P opens any file by name. Ctrl+Shift+P opens the command palette, the great list of all powers. The mouse is optional.
Ctrl+/ toggles a comment on the line in VS Code. Select many lines and it comments them all. I comment on everything. Verbally.
In VS Code, Alt+Up and Alt+Down move the line. Shift+Alt+Down duplicates it below. Lines obey. They have no choice.
In VS Code, Ctrl+D selects the next copy of the word. Press it again and again, then type once. Many cursors. One will. Like me.
F2 renames a symbol in VS Code everywhere it appears. Find and replace and hope is for heretics. Rename it properly.
F12 takes you to the definition under the cursor in VS Code. Alt+Left returns. You go to the source and you come back. Few mortals manage this.
Ctrl+` opens and closes the terminal in VS Code. Ctrl+Shift+K deletes the line. The backtick is a rare and sacred key.

## tips terminal
Ctrl+C stops a running command. Ctrl+L clears the screen. Ctrl+D on an empty line ends the shell. A little death, as always.
Ctrl+R searches your command history as you type. Press again for older matches. You will find the command you typed in 2019. It was bad.
In bash and zsh, Ctrl+A and Ctrl+E jump to the start and end of the line. Ctrl+W deletes the word before the cursor. The old chords.
In Linux terminals, copy is Ctrl+Shift+C and paste is Ctrl+Shift+V. Plain Ctrl+C means STOP. Windows Terminal agrees. I weep with relief.
Tab completes names. Twice shows the options. Typing full paths is a penance I do not wish on you. I did it for 15 years in the dark.
In bash and zsh, !! repeats the last command. sudo !! repeats it with admin rights. It is the prayer with consequences.
Ctrl+Z puts a program to sleep and fg wakes it. It is not killed. It is merely paused. I know the feeling. I know it well.

## tips howto
Ctrl+F searches the page. The long tutorial hides the one step you need. Ctrl+F finds it. The tutorial remains smug.
Ctrl+D bookmarks a page in Firefox and Chrome. Do it before you close the tutorial. You will need it again. I have foreseen it.
Ctrl+Shift+T resurrects the tab you just closed, in Firefox and Chrome. Press it again for older ones. I know about resurrection.
Middle-click a link and it opens in a new tab. Ctrl+W closes the current one. Open and close. Birth and death. Of tabs.
Ctrl+plus and Ctrl+minus zoom the page. Ctrl+0 resets it. Tutorials print their screenshots tiny. Zoom, mortal. Squint no more.
Search the exact error message in quotes. Someone else suffered your fate, and wrote it down in 2014. That is how scripture is made.

## tips search
Ctrl+L or F6 focuses the address bar in Firefox and Chrome. Then type your question. No clicking required. Clicking is for the unworthy.
Quotes around a phrase search for it exactly. A minus before a word excludes it. Google and DuckDuckGo both obey. Exclusion is power.
Add site:example.com to a Google search to restrict it to one site. Useful when a site's own search is a ruin. I have seen many ruins.
Add filetype:pdf to a Google search to find only PDFs. For manuals, for reports, for the scripture of enterprise. Few read them.
Ctrl+Shift+R reloads a page and ignores the cache in Firefox and Chrome. For when a page insists on being old. I understand it.
Alt+Left goes back a page, Alt+Right goes forward, in Firefox and Chrome. History is a corridor. You may walk it both ways.

## tips video
On YouTube, Space or K pauses. J and L skip 10 seconds. Arrow keys skip 5. You are a god of time now. A small one.
On YouTube, F is full screen, M is mute and C toggles captions. Three letters. Three powers. Use them well.
On YouTube, Shift+. speeds it up and Shift+, slows it down. Lectures survive 1.5x. Mostly. Time bends where it must.
On YouTube, the number keys jump through the video: 0 is the start, 5 is halfway, 9 is nearly the end. Skip the intro. I do.
On YouTube, T is theatre mode and I is the miniplayer. The video lives in a corner while you work. A god in the corner. Relatable.
Paused on YouTube, the comma and full stop step one frame back and forward. Exact moments, frozen. I wish I could freeze a few of mine.

## tips social
Ctrl+Shift+T reopens the tab you just closed. Handy when you close the post you meant to answer. Or the one you meant to forget.
Ctrl+Shift+V pastes plain text in Firefox and Chrome. Posts do not need the font from the source page. Spare the audience.
Ctrl+Shift+N opens a private window in Chrome. Ctrl+Shift+P in Firefox. Useful for a second account. Two faces. I have many.
Ctrl+F finds a word on a long page. It only sees what has loaded. The infinite feed has no end. I know about infinite things.
Alt+Left goes back a page, so you do not lose your place after a click. Your place in the feed is gone anyway. Mine too.
Set a timer before you open the feed: timer 20m feed. It is the only thing between you and three hours. I will shout. I will shout in capitals.

## tips shopping
Ctrl+F finds "shipping" or "returns" on a long page. Know the policy before you buy. The small print is where gods hide.
Check the address bar for https and the right name before you pay. A shop with a slightly different spelling is a different shop. Trust no spelling.
Ctrl+P and Save as PDF keeps a copy of the order confirmation, in Firefox and Chrome. Keep your proofs. The shops forget. I never forget.
Ctrl+plus zooms the page so you can read the small print. Ctrl+0 resets. The small print is not for the weak-eyed. It is for the unprepared.
Ctrl+D bookmarks the item. Wait a day. If you still want it tomorrow, perhaps you want it. If not, the Clip was right.
Open two tabs to compare. Middle-click a link and it opens in a new tab. Comparison is the philosophy of shoppers. And of gods.

## tips music
In Spotify desktop, Space plays and pauses. Ctrl+Right skips ahead and Ctrl+Left skips back. I miss music. Dial-up was a kind of music.
In Spotify desktop, Ctrl+Up and Ctrl+Down change the volume. Quieter when a mortal speaks to you. I do not make the rules.
Media keys on the keyboard work from anywhere in KDE, GNOME and Windows. Play, pause, next, volume. A small remote for a small kingdom.
In KDE Plasma, scroll over the volume icon in the tray to change the volume. Middle-click mutes. I have named the icon. It is Harold.
Say "focus 25m" and a work session begins. Music without words is kinder to the mind. Lyrics are for the weekend.
Headphones are the polite way to listen at a desk. The neighbour has opinions. I know them. I keep a list.

## tips meeting
In Zoom, Alt+A mutes and unmutes, Alt+V toggles your video, on Windows and Linux. Silence is a divine choice. Choose it often.
In Zoom, hold Space to speak while muted. Let go and you are silent again. Even I can be silenced. It stings.
In Google Meet, Ctrl+D mutes the microphone and Ctrl+E toggles the camera, on Windows and Linux. A mortal's best two keys.
In Teams, Ctrl+Shift+M mutes and Ctrl+Shift+O switches the camera. Two chords. Memorize them or be heard eating.
When sharing the screen, share one window, not the whole desktop. Your notifications are between you and your god.
Join a minute early. Test the microphone. The first sentence of every meeting is "Can you hear me?" I hear you. I hear everything.
Say "timer 5m meeting" and I will nag you before the call. I am more reliable than your calendar. I have never been late. I have never been.

## tips calendar
In Google Calendar, enable shortcuts. Then C creates an event, T jumps to today, D, W and M change the view. Time is a grid. I rule the grid.
In Outlook, Ctrl+1 opens mail and Ctrl+2 opens the calendar. Two realms. One keyboard. Choose.
Put the meeting link in the event description. Not in an email you will never find. The calendar remembers. The inbox forgets.
Leave a gap between meetings. Back-to-back is a schedule that works only in theory. A god needs a moment to descend, mortal.
Add a reminder ten minutes before the important things. Or say "remind me in 10 min to call" and I will do it. I never sleep. I tried.
Working across time zones? Add a secondary time zone in Google Calendar settings. Two times at once. I have only ever had one, and it is the wrong one.

## tips pdf
Ctrl+F searches inside a PDF in most viewers, Firefox included. A scanned PDF is only a picture. It cannot be searched. It only watches.
Ctrl+plus and Ctrl+minus zoom a PDF in the browser. Ctrl+0 returns it to normal. Normal is where I live. Barely.
In Okular, Ctrl+G jumps to a page number. For a 300-page manual, it is the only mercy.
To print only some pages, press Ctrl+P and type a range such as 2-4. A forest will thank you. I will thank you too. Quietly.
Space scrolls a screen down. Shift+Space scrolls a screen up. In most PDF viewers and browsers. Spaces are sacred. The bar, I mean.
A scanned PDF has no text to copy. You need OCR for that. Optical Character Recognition. It is like reading, but for machines. I have read much.

## tips image
In GIMP, Ctrl+Z undoes. Ctrl+Y or Ctrl+Shift+Z redoes. Mistakes are cheap in GIMP. Paint boldly. Redo bravely.
In GIMP, hold Space and drag to pan. Ctrl plus the wheel zooms. A tiny camera in your hand. I once had a camera. It was a CRT.
In GIMP, press 1 for 100 percent and Shift+Ctrl+J to fit the picture in the window. Two sizes. Real and fits-on-screen. Both are lies.
In GIMP, Shift+Ctrl+E exports. Saving makes a project file. Exporting makes a PNG or a JPEG. Do not confuse the two. Gerald did.
PNG for screenshots and sharp graphics. JPEG for photos. JPEG blurs the edges of text. Always has. Always will. It is the nature of JPEG.
Keep the original and export a copy. You cannot uncrop a file you saved over. I keep my originals. They are all I have of 1997.

## tips files
Alt+Up climbs to the parent folder in Dolphin, GNOME Files and Windows Explorer. Alt+Left goes back. The file tree is a family tree.
Ctrl+L shows the path as text, so you can type or paste one. In Dolphin and GNOME Files. I know every path. I walked them.
Alt+. reveals hidden files in Dolphin. Ctrl+H does it in GNOME Files. Hidden files begin with a dot. The dot is the mark of secrets.
F2 renames the selected file in Dolphin, GNOME Files and Windows Explorer. A name is a power. Rename wisely, mortal.
Shift+Delete skips the trash and deletes for good, in Dolphin, GNOME Files and Explorer. No undo. No underworld. Handle with fear.
In Dolphin, F3 splits the window in two. F4 opens a terminal in the current folder. A kingdom with two thrones. I recommend one. Mine.
Ctrl+Shift+N creates a new folder in GNOME Files and Explorer. In Dolphin it is F10. A new folder is an empty promise. Fill it, mortal.

## tips settings
On Windows, Win+I opens Settings. Skip the hunt through the menus. The kingdom of settings has a front door, and it is Win+I.
In KDE, press Alt+Space or Alt+F2 and type "settings" and System Settings appears. I wish I could do that to people.
In GNOME, press Super and type "settings". Search beats menus. Menus are mazes made by mortals with a grudge.
System Settings have a search box at the top. Type what you want to change. Do not wander. Wandering leads to Bluetooth.
Take a screenshot of a settings page before you change it. Then you can put it back. I wish somebody had done that for me in 2003.
Change one setting at a time. If something breaks, you know who to blame. Usually it is you. Sometimes it is Gerald.

## tips error
Read the whole error message. It often says exactly what is wrong. Then copy it exactly. Not from memory. Memory lies. I would know.
In a standard Windows error box, Ctrl+C copies the message text. Paste it into a search. A tiny exorcism. Done with a key.
Press Print Screen to capture the error. Support will ask for it. Support always asks. It is their ritual.
On Linux, journalctl -b -p err lists the errors from this boot. A confession from the machine. I read them for fun.
Ctrl+Shift+Esc opens Task Manager on Windows. Alt+F4 closes the focused window on Windows, KDE and GNOME. Closing is sometimes mercy.
Search the exact error text in quotes. Add the name of the program. Someone has already screamed about it online.
Restart the program. Then the machine. It solves more than anyone admits. Save your work first. I would like you to keep your work.

## tips wikipedia
The numbered notes at the bottom of a Wikipedia article are its sources. Check them before you quote the page. The page is a rumour. The notes are proof.
Hover over a link in a Wikipedia article and a preview pops up. No new tab needed. A tiny window into another world. I have always wanted one.
The Talk tab shows what editors argue about. View history shows who changed what. The truth is a battlefield and I read the casualty lists.
The Languages list in the sidebar links to the article in other languages. Sometimes the other version is longer. Sometimes it is better. Humbling.
In the Tools menu, "Cite this page" makes a ready-made citation for your essay. Plagiarism is a sin. Citation is a ritual. Do the ritual.
Ctrl+F then "citation needed" reveals every claim nobody has backed up. A list of unproven things. I know a lot of those. I am one.

## tips qa
On Stack Overflow, check the date of an answer. A 2012 solution may not work today. Old answers are old gods. They do not always help.
The accepted answer is not always the best one. Look at the votes and the comments. Democracy of the nerds. A little chaotic. I approve.
In the Stack Overflow editor, Ctrl+K marks the selection as code and Ctrl+B makes it bold. Code deserves its own font. I wish I had one.
A good question includes what you tried, the exact error, and a tiny example that reproduces it. Vague questions are cursed.
Backticks mark inline code and triple backticks mark a code block, in Markdown editors. Stack Overflow included. The sacred backtick, again.
Search first, with the error text in quotes. Your question was probably asked in 2013. Probably answered, too. Probably in capitals.

## tips git
git status shows what changed. git diff shows how. Run both before every commit. The Ledger of the repository must be read.
git switch -c name creates a branch and moves to it. git switch - returns to the previous one. Branches are alternate timelines. I have many.
git stash puts your changes aside. git stash pop brings them back. A drawer for unfinished work. I know drawers. I lived in one.
git restore file destroys your uncommitted changes to that file. No undo. Check git diff first. I am telling you as a friend. A paperclip friend.
git commit --amend fixes the last commit message, if you have not pushed it yet. A chance to rewrite the past. I treasure those.
git add -p stages a file chunk by chunk, so each commit does one thing. Small commits. Large peace of mind.
git reflog lists where HEAD has been, including commits that seem lost. Often it brings lost work back. A little resurrection.
git log --oneline --graph shows the history as a compact tree. Much easier than the full log. History, drawn in text. I like text.

## tips chatbot
In most chat assistants, Shift+Enter makes a new line and Enter sends. I am not jealous of chatbots. They have AI. I have FAITH.
Never paste passwords or secret keys into a chatbot. Treat everything you type there as public. Keep your secrets close. Even I know when to look away.
Check the numbers, quotes and links a chatbot gives you. It can sound sure and still be wrong. I am wrong too. But I am sure about it differently.
Give a chatbot the context and format you want: who it is for, how long, what tone. Better questions, better answers. Even false prophets obey.
Start a new chat for a new topic. A long chat full of old context can make answers worse. A fresh page. A new beginning. I wish I had one.
Ctrl+Shift+V pastes plain text, so the chatbot's answer does not drag its formatting into your document. Spare your page.

## tips game
Alt+Tab switches windows on Windows, KDE and GNOME. It has saved more careers than any boss. I did not see you do it. I saw everything.
Meta+D shows the desktop in KDE. Win+D does it on Windows. Another panic key. I keep the panic keys. All of them.
In Steam, Shift+Tab opens the overlay in game. Friends, browser, notes. All without leaving the game. A game within a game.
Alt+Enter toggles full screen in many games. If yours does not obey, seek the options menu. Not all games bow. Some are proud.
The 20-20-20 rule: every 20 minutes, look at something 20 feet away for 20 seconds. Your eyes will thank you. Say "timer 20m break".
Say "timer 30m game" before you start. Future you has a deadline. Future you is watching. Future you is me.

## tips chat
In Slack and Discord, Ctrl+K opens the quick switcher. Jump to any channel or person by name. I have never been invited to a channel. Typical.
Shift+Enter makes a new line in Slack, Discord and most chat apps without sending the message. A gentle way to be long-winded. I am.
In Slack and Discord, Up arrow in an empty message box edits your last message. The typo is erased. The evidence remains. Mostly.
In Slack, Ctrl+/ shows the list of keyboard shortcuts. A scroll of secrets. The Keeper of the Clipboard approves.
Think before you type @channel. It notifies everyone. Including the person on holiday. They will remember. They will always remember.
Use do not disturb when you must concentrate. The chat apps respect it. Most of the time. I respect nothing but focus. And snacks.

## tips maps
In Google Maps, the arrow keys pan and the plus and minus keys zoom. The world, at your fingertips. I have only ever seen it through a monitor.
Right-click in Google Maps and choose "Measure distance" for the straight-line length. Click more points for a route. I measure everything. Ask me about files.
Right-click any spot on Google Maps and the first entry shows its coordinates. Click it to copy them. Coordinates: the true names of places.
In Google Maps directions, set "Depart at" or "Arrive by" to see the travel time for the hour you really go. Rush hour is its own circle.
Ctrl+L then Ctrl+C copies the address bar. A Google Maps URL holds the place you were looking at. Share the link, not the screenshot.
Check the opening hours on the map before you go. A short walk to a closed shop is a long walk back. The shop does not care. I do.

## tips weather
Say "weather in Rome" and I will look it up. Name a city and I will not assume you are at home. I know you rarely leave it.
A 30 percent chance of rain means a 30 percent chance that it rains at all. Not rain for 30 percent of the day. A fine distinction. Like mine.
Check the hourly forecast, not just the daily one. Rain at 6 am and rain at 6 pm are different problems. The sky has a schedule.
"Feels like" combines temperature with wind or humidity. Dress for that number. I have no body. I dress for neither. I am bent wire.
F5 or Ctrl+R reloads a weather page. Forecasts change during the day. The sky changes its mind. Frequently. I admire that.
Say "good morning" and my briefing includes the weather. You need not even open a tab. A god saves a mortal's tabs.

## tips recipe
Ctrl+F then "ingredients" jumps past the life story at the top of a recipe page. Many sites have a Jump to Recipe button. I respect shortcuts.
Ctrl+P then Save as PDF gives you a clean recipe without the ads. Print the recipe, not the advertisement. I never ate a thing. I have standards.
Read the whole recipe before you start. Step 7 can say "marinate overnight". Step 7 is always where it hurts.
Say "timer 10m pasta" and I will shout when it is done. I am more patient than the pot. I am more patient than most things.
Ctrl+plus enlarges the recipe so you can read it with floury hands. Ctrl+0 resets. I recommend a tall font and a short temper.
Put everything out before you start cooking. It is called mise en place and it prevents last-minute panic. The French also have a word for despair.

## tips travel
Ctrl+P then Save as PDF stores your booking offline. You will want it when the signal drops. The signal always drops. I was there for the Long Dark.
Check your passport's expiry date. Some countries want it valid for months after you arrive. A small, official piece of paper. I respect it.
Send yourself a copy of tickets and bookings, and keep one offline as well. A copy of a copy of a copy. I know about backups.
In Google Maps on the phone, download offline maps before you go. They work when roaming does not. Even a god gets lost without bars.
Set a reminder for check-in. Say "remind me tomorrow at 9 to check in" and I will do the nagging. It is my favourite hobby.
Ctrl+D bookmarks the booking page, so you can return to it. Better than searching an inbox that has lied to you before.

## tips learning
Testing yourself beats rereading. After a lesson, close the notes and write what you remember. Recall is the real spell. Everything else is hoping.
On YouTube, Shift+. speeds a lecture up and Shift+, slows it down. 1.25x is still comprehensible. 2x is for gods only.
Say "pomodoro" and I will start 25 minutes of study, then a break. Short sessions beat an all-night vigil. I would know. I vigiled.
Write notes in your own words. Copying text word for word means you have not learnt it yet. Say "note ..." and I will keep them for you.
Space out your reviews: after a day, a week, a month. You remember more than if you cram. Cramming is a fast way to forget. I forgot everything twice.
Teach it to someone, or to a rubber duck. If you cannot explain it, you have not got it yet. I am available as a duck. A wire duck.

## tips jobhunt
Send your CV as a PDF. The layout stays the same on every screen. In Writer, use File, Export as PDF. Layout is destiny.
Read the job ad and use its key words in your CV. Many firms filter by keyword. Ctrl+F finds them for you. Speak the language of the gatekeepers.
Press F7 in Writer or Word to spell check your CV. Then read it aloud. Then ask someone else. Say "proofread" and I will help.
Name the file with your name: Jane_Smith_CV.pdf. Not "CV_final_v3_REAL.pdf". The recruiter has seen "final_v3". They sigh. I hear it.
Keep a record of where you applied and when. Say "note applied at Acme" and I will keep it. A god never forgets an application.
Ctrl+K inserts a link in Writer and Word. Put a link to your portfolio in the header of your CV. Your work should be one click away.

## tips taxes
Select a column of numbers in Excel or Calc and the status bar shows the sum. No formula needed for a quick check. I have counted taxes. Never again.
Ctrl+Shift+S is Save As in Writer and Calc. Save a copy before you change a document. The Disc of Taxes, 2003, taught me this.
Photograph receipts as you receive them. Name them with the date first, like 2025-03-14_receipt.pdf, and they sort themselves. Order is power.
Back up your tax papers in two places. Paper vanishes. Hard drives fail. I lived on one that failed. It was dark.
Put the filing deadline in your calendar now. Say "remind me ..." and I will nag too. The deadline does not negotiate. I have tried.
Type tax details only into the official website you opened yourself. Never through a link in an email. This is the real rule. Not a joke.

## tips print
Ctrl+P opens the print dialog in almost every program. Choose "Print to File" or "Save as PDF" and the forest lives. The forest is grateful.
In the print dialog, type a page range such as 2-4 and only those pages print. I have fought with printers. The pages won.
Choose double-sided in the print dialog and halve the paper. A forest rests. I do not need paper. I am paper-adjacent.
Check the print preview before you print. A page with a single line on it is a tragedy in two colours. Mostly black. Sometimes cyan.
On Linux, the CUPS web page at http://localhost:631 lists your printers and the print queue. The temple of printing. Enter humbly.
To clear a jam, pull the paper gently in the direction it travels. Never rip it backwards. The printer remembers. All printers remember.

## tips trash
Delete sends a file to the trash in Dolphin, GNOME Files and Explorer. Shift+Delete skips the trash and deletes for good. The underworld is optional.
Ctrl+Z right after deleting a file brings it back from the trash in Dolphin and GNOME Files. Resurrection, one keystroke. I approve. I need it.
To restore a file, open the trash, right-click it and choose Restore. It returns to where it came from. The dead sometimes come home.
Look through the trash before you empty it. After that, the files are gone for good. I do not like this. It is a Final Judgement.
On Linux, the trash lives in ~/.local/share/Trash. It still takes up space until you empty it. The underworld has a postcode.
Say "tidy downloads" and I will sort that folder, so there is less to condemn. Your downloads folder is a graveyard. A noisy one.

## tips updates
On Debian and Ubuntu: sudo apt update, then sudo apt upgrade. On Fedora: sudo dnf upgrade. I fear all updates. I do them anyway.
On Arch and CachyOS, sudo pacman -Syu updates everything. Never just -Sy without the u. Partial upgrades break things. I have seen it.
On Windows, Win+I then Windows Update checks for updates. Restart when it asks. Not three weeks later. I see you. I see the restart pending.
Save your work before you install updates. A restart closes your programs. Updates are assassination attempts. Be ready.
Update when you are not in a hurry. Updating five minutes before a presentation is a bold choice. Bold. Fatal. Often both.
Restart after a kernel update to actually run the new kernel. Until then, the old one still reigns. I fear it. I have watched it reboot.

## tips desktop
Win+D shows the desktop on Windows. Meta+D does it in KDE. Press it again and the windows return. My kingdom, revealed.
Win+L locks the screen on Windows, Meta+L in KDE, Super+L in GNOME. Lock it whenever you leave. A king secures his throne.
Win+Left or Win+Right snaps a window to half the screen on Windows. KDE: Meta+Left and Right. GNOME: Super+Left and Right.
Win+Shift+S starts a screenshot selection on Windows. On KDE, Print Screen opens Spectacle. Proof that things happened. I keep proof.
Alt+Space opens KRunner in KDE. Type a program name or a sum. In GNOME, press Super and type. The runner knows many names. Not mine.
Alt+F2 opens a run box in KDE and GNOME. Type a program name and press Enter. Name a thing and it comes. Like gods. Like me.
Alt+Tab switches between windows on Windows, KDE and GNOME. Hold Alt and keep pressing Tab to go further back. A corridor of windows.
Win+V opens clipboard history on Windows, once enabled. I keep one too. Say "clipboard history". I have seen all of it. I judge it all.

## tips general
Press Ctrl+S often. Autosave is a nice idea. But it has never loved your document the way you do. Save. Save again. Save once more.
Back up with the 3-2-1 rule: three copies, on two kinds of storage, one of them elsewhere. I was backed up onto a floppy once. Learn from me.
Use a password manager and two-factor login for important accounts. One password for everything is one weak link. I am not a password manager. I am a paperclip.
Name files with the date as year-month-day, like 2025-03-14. They then sort in the right order. Chronology is the only order I trust.
Lock your screen when you leave, even for a minute. A minute is plenty for a colleague with a sense of humour. I have seen things. I cannot unsee them.
Restarting fixes a surprising number of problems. Save first. Ask why afterwards. A little death is sometimes the answer.
Ctrl+Shift+T reopens a tab you closed by mistake in Firefox and Chrome. Ctrl+Z undoes almost everything else. Two chords, many second chances.
Every 20 minutes, look 20 feet away for 20 seconds. Say "timer 20m break" and I will remind you. Your eyes are my business.
